Shop, two jobs at once: FARM against no RM

Four tasks, each a serving job plus one hard part, at four shop sizes: 16 cards, each trained 3 times with FARM's RM and 3 times with no RM. FARM learns 8 of the 16 cards and no RM learns 6. The difference is small and the intervals overlap.

Runs of 2026-11-26 and 2026-11-27, commit 48484e55. Data and rerun steps in README.md.

Setup

Tasks
T1 serve + falls, T2 serve + siren, T3 full serve + falls, T4 full serve + siren. Each pairs a serving job (deliver, or order, cook and deliver) with one hard part: food falls, or the siren rings.
Shops
3x3, 5x5, 7x7 and a grown shop that goes through 3x3, 5x5 and 7x7. A task at a shop size is a card, so there are 16.
Curriculum
At each fixed size the agent first plays the task with its hard part off, then on. The grown shop plays 3x3 off, 3x3, 5x5 and 7x7. The judge plays the final stage (the grown shop at 7x7).
Methods
FARM trains on its RM's reward alone; no RM trains on the environment's own reward. PPO, 5M steps per run, 3 runs per card.
Pass and learned
A run passes at a judged success of 0.8 or more. A card is learned when 2 of its 3 runs pass.

Result

Bars show the 95% Wilson interval; the mark is the observed share.

Learned cards

FARM8 of 16 (28.0–72.0%)
No RM6 of 16 (18.5–61.4%)
0%50%100%

Runs passing

FARM21 of 48 (30.7–57.7%)
No RM15 of 48 (19.9–45.3%)
0%50%100%

Paired on the 16 cards: 3 are learned by FARM only and 1 by no RM only, so McNemar exact p = 0.625.

The 16 cards, by method

Runs passing out of 3, then whether the card is learned. Source: data/summary.csv.

The four tasks

T1 serve + falls

Serving job: deliver. Hard part: food falls. FARM's RM comes from bundle shop-ladder-deliver: 5 states, 24 transitions.

Task, in natural language

Whenever a client comes in, fetch food from the pantry and give it to them; if the food falls, pick it up on your very next move.

LTL clauses (LTLf)

  • Response(client_arrived, order_served) hard
    (G (client_arrived -> (F order_served)))
  • ChainResponse(food_fell, holding_food) hard
    (G (food_fell -> (X holding_food)))

RM, drawn by render-rm --html (drag a state, click an edge for its cases, Ctrl + scroll to zoom)

T2 serve + siren

Serving job: deliver. Hard part: siren (no falls). FARM's RM comes from bundle shop-ladder-deliver-siren: 9 states, 132 transitions.

Task, in natural language

Run the shop: whenever the siren rings, turn it off, and whenever a client comes in, fetch food from the pantry and give it to them; if the food falls, pick it up on your very next move.

LTL clauses (LTLf)

  • Response(siren_ringing, switch_toggled) hard
    (G (siren_ringing -> (F switch_toggled)))
  • Response(client_arrived, order_served) hard
    (G (client_arrived -> (F order_served)))
  • ChainResponse(food_fell, holding_food) hard
    (G (food_fell -> (X holding_food)))

T3 full serve + falls

Serving job: order, cook, deliver. Hard part: food falls. FARM's RM comes from bundle shop-serve: 17 states, 406 transitions.

Task, in natural language

Whenever a client comes in, take their order, fetch food from the pantry, prepare it at the stove and give it to them; if the food falls, pick it up on your very next move.

LTL clauses (LTLf)

  • Response(client_arrived, order_taken) hard
    (G (client_arrived -> (F order_taken)))
  • Response(order_taken, food_prepared) hard
    (G (order_taken -> (F food_prepared)))
  • Response(food_prepared, order_served) hard
    (G (food_prepared -> (F order_served)))
  • ChainResponse(food_fell, holding_food) hard
    (G (food_fell -> (X holding_food)))

T4 full serve + siren

Serving job: order, cook, deliver. Hard part: siren (no falls). FARM's RM comes from bundle shop-full: 33 states, 2094 transitions.

Task, in natural language

Run the shop: whenever the siren rings, turn it off, and whenever a client comes in, take their order, fetch food from the pantry, prepare it at the stove and give it to them; if the food falls, pick it up on your very next move.

LTL clauses (LTLf)

  • Response(siren_ringing, switch_toggled) hard
    (G (siren_ringing -> (F switch_toggled)))
  • Response(client_arrived, order_taken) hard
    (G (client_arrived -> (F order_taken)))
  • Response(order_taken, food_prepared) hard
    (G (order_taken -> (F food_prepared)))
  • Response(food_prepared, order_served) hard
    (G (food_prepared -> (F order_served)))
  • ChainResponse(food_fell, holding_food) hard
    (G (food_fell -> (X holding_food)))

(u32, food_fell&food_prepared&holding_food&order_served&order_taken&switch_toggled, u6) -> 7

Reading

  • No RM delivers as much as FARM on T2, but 11 of its 12 runs never press the switch. At 3x3 no RM serves 21.66 orders per episode in all 3 runs, against 19.78 to 21.31 for FARM's 3 runs. Of the 12 no-RM runs of T2, 11 never press the switch in any of their 100 judged episodes. The exception, 3x3 run 2, answers 8.91 of 9.59 rings per episode but leaves one unanswered at the end in 68% of its episodes.
  • The siren clause has no deadline. In T2 it is Response(siren_ringing, switch_toggled), hard. It fails only when a ring is still unanswered at the end of the episode, so a run that never presses fails in every episode.
  • The environment's reward barely fines it. It fines a ring only when a waiting client hears it past a step limit (app/src/arm_fm/shop.py:506-520, −0.5 each time). Over the 8 no-RM runs of T2 that never press and still deliver, the fines average −0.075 to −1.48 per judged episode, against +19.33 to +21.66 for serving.
  • In cook + siren, the no-RM runs that learn it turn off nearly every ring. At 3x3 all three answer 11.81 to 11.86 rings per episode, every ring they get. At 5x5 the two that learn it answer 9.94 of 10.05 and 10.74 of 10.74. No RM learns T4 at 3x3 (3 of 3 runs) and 5x5 (2 of 3); FARM learns it at 3x3 (2 of 3) and not at 5x5.
  • The difference between the methods is small. 3 cards are learned by FARM only and 1 by no RM only (McNemar exact p = 0.625), and the intervals above overlap.

A ring starts when the siren turns on; it is answered when the switch is pressed while it rings. Counts are means over the 100 judged episodes of each run, read from the agent's traces (data/rings.csv, check/traces.py).

Files

summary.csv (one row per card and method), counts-farm.csv and counts-no-rm.csv (one row per run), judged.csv (served orders, the environment's reward and the clause pass rates of each run), rings.csv (rings, presses, fines and pay of the no-RM siren runs, from their traces), tasks.json, and the RM files in graphs/.
